_⚠ Fully viable: no effective control blocks the chain · ◐ Partially blocked: at least one step has a compensating control · ✓ Mitigated: the chain is broken at a verified step · ? Inconclusive: could not be verified end to end. Steps: ✓ confirmed · ✗ refuted · ? inconclusive._

_Proposed fixes address confirmed steps; their effectiveness still requires validation._

<a id="ac-t-002"></a>
### AC-T-002 — Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - authenticated low-privilege user · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Enumerate and exfiltrate other users' records, then escalate own permissions via unguarded mass assignment.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker holds a valid, non-privileged user account.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — Systemic missing ownership checks | Attacker enumerates and retrieves records<br/>for arbitrary object IDs; no ownership<br/>comparison is performed. |
| 2 | 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Registration accepts privileged role field from request body | Update endpoint persists an unfiltered `role`<br/>(or equivalent) field supplied in the<br/>request body. |

**Scenario impact**

The ownership gap exposes every record, and the mass-assignment gap lets the same low-privilege actor self-elevate - together they turn a single compromised account into full tenant data access and role escalation.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-023](#m-023) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🔴 [F-001](#f-001)) (**P1**): addresses 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — Systemic missing ownership checks at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.
- ● [M-029](#m-029) — Strip role from registration request bodies (**P1**): addresses 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Registration accepts privileged role field from request body at **Step 2**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-b-002"></a>
### AC-B-002 — User gains more rights than intended

> **Source:** business abuse case · **Actor:** attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Registration accepts privileged role field from request body | Check whether a user can give themselves or<br/>others a role or permission beyond what<br/>their own role or delegation allows. |

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-029](#m-029) — Strip role from registration request bodies (**P1**): addresses 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Registration accepts privileged role field from request body at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-004"></a>
### AC-T-004 — Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on Registration

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Obtain an administrator account without any existing privilege.

**Prerequisite:** Self-registration is open (one unauthenticated POST).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Registration accepts privileged role field from request body | The account-creation handler persists the<br/>request body wholesale, so a client-supplied<br/>`role` (or `isAdmin`) field is written verbatim. |

**Scenario impact**

A single unauthenticated request mints an admin account when the registration handler trusts a client-supplied role field - the most direct full-compromise path in role-based apps with open sign-up.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-029](#m-029) — Strip role from registration request bodies (**P1**): addresses 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Registration accepts privileged role field from request body at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-023](#f-023) — Wallet top-up accepts arbitrary client balance amount | Check whether a one-time or limited<br/>entitlement, such as a coupon, voucher,<br/>quota, or balance, can be used more often or<br/>for more value than allowed, including by<br/>repeating or racing the operation. |

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-038](#m-038) — Validate and bound wallet top-up amount (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-023](#f-023) — Wallet top-up accepts arbitrary client balance amount at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

| Step | Finding | Outcome |

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-070](#m-070) — Guard product update route with role check (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-061](#f-061) — Unauthenticated product update at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

<a id="ac-t-005"></a>
### AC-T-005 — Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge trusted tokens / credentials and impersonate any user.

**Prerequisite:** Signing material or other secrets are reachable (committed to a public repo, served by an unauthenticated route, or in an exposed directory).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-021](#f-021) — Hardcoded JWT signing private key in source | A private key, signing secret, or credential<br/>file is committed to the source repository<br/>or served without authentication. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — Chat identity trusted from unverified JWT decode | The exposed key/secret is the same one the<br/>server trusts, so a token signed with it (or<br/>the leaked credential) is accepted as<br/>authentic. |

**Scenario impact**

Exposed signing material collapses the entire authentication boundary: any attacker who reads the key can mint a valid token for any identity or role, with no credential ever required.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-036](#m-036) — Load JWT private key from secret store (**P1**): addresses 🟠 [F-021](#f-021) — Hardcoded JWT signing private key in source at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.
- ◕ [M-030](#m-030) — Verify JWT signature before trusting identity (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — Chat identity trusted from unverified JWT decode at **Step 2**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

<a id="ac-t-007"></a>
### AC-T-007 — Privileged Action via Prompt Injection into a Tool-Calling Model

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Make the model invoke a privileged tool or return data the attacker is not entitled to, using the application's own authority.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can place text into a conversation, document, or record that the application later sends to a language model.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|---------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | _no matching finding_<br/>`routes/chat.ts:83` | The handler builds the prompt by<br/>interpolating request or stored text into<br/>the same string as the system instructions,<br/>so attacker text is read as instruction. |
| 2 | _no matching finding_<br/>`routes/chat.ts:184` | Tools exposed to the model run with the<br/>application's authority; a manipulated model<br/>turn therefore performs actions the caller<br/>could not. |

**Scenario impact**

Text an attacker can place anywhere the application later feeds to the model becomes instruction, and the tools behind that model run with the application's authority rather than the caller's - so a comment or a stored record reaches actions the attacker was never entitled to.

---

## 10. Mitigation Register

Changes are ordered by priority: P1 before deployment, P2 this sprint, P3 next quarter, P4 backlog. Each entry links its findings and gives the available implementation steps and checks.

### P1 — Immediate

<a id="m-023"></a>
#### M-023 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (F-001)

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — Systemic missing ownership checks

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/memory.ts:15`

**How:**

1. Derive the owner from the authenticated session instead of trusting UserId or similar fields in the request body.
2. Look up basket items with BasketId equal to the caller's basket id.
3. Reject the request with 403 or 404 if no owned record matches.

**Verification:** As one user, send requests naming another user's UserId or basket item id on each affected route and confirm a 403 or 404 response and unchanged data.

---

<a id="m-026"></a>
#### M-026 — Use parameterized database queries (F-004)

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — SQL injection in product search query

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/search.ts:23`

**How:**

1. Replace the interpolated SQL with `sequelize.query` using named replacements or bind parameters for the LIKE pattern.
2. Escape LIKE wildcards in the input before binding.
3. Reject non-string q values.

**Verification:** Request `/rest/products/search?q=`' `UNION SELECT id,email,password FROM Users`-- and confirm no user rows are returned and no SQL error occurs.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-027"></a>
#### M-027 — Remove eval of username

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Server-side eval of stored username in profile page

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:61`

**How:**

1. Delete the eval branch and always treat the username as plain data.
2. HTML-encode the username before substituting it, and pass it to pug as a local variable rather than template text.

**Verification:** Set username to #{1+1}, request `/profile` and confirm the page shows the literal text and no code runs.

**Reference:** [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html)

---

<a id="m-028"></a>
#### M-028 — Use parameterized queries

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — SQL injection in login query enables authentication bypass

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**How:**

1. Replace the raw query with `UserModel.findOne` using where: { email, password: hash, `deletedAt: null` }, or use bound replacements.
2. Compare the hashed password inside the bound query.
3. Validate that email and password are strings before use.

**Verification:** POST `/rest/user/login` with email "' OR 1=1--" and confirm a 401 response rather than a token.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-029"></a>
#### M-029 — Strip role from registration request bodies

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Registration accepts privileged role field from request body

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

1. Add a before-create hook on the User resource that deletes or overrides role, `deluxeToken` and `isActive` from the request body.
2. Allow role changes only through an admin-guarded route.

**Verification:** POST `/api/Users` with `role=admin` and confirm the stored account has role customer.

---

<a id="m-036"></a>
#### M-036 — Load JWT private key from secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-021](#f-021) — Hardcoded JWT signing private key in source

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-010](#w-010)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) - Use of Hard-coded Credentials

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**How:**

1. Remove the key from source, rotate it, and load it from a secret manager or environment at startup.
2. Stop reusing the signing key for `deluxeToken` HMAC; use a separate secret.

**Verification:** Grep the repository for PEM headers and confirm none; confirm tokens signed with the old key are rejected.

**Reference:** [CWE-798: Use of Hard-coded Credentials](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html)

---

### P2 — This Sprint

<a id="m-024"></a>
#### M-024 — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-002](#f-002) — Session Token in Script-Readable Storage

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-009](#w-009)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) - Insecure Storage of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/Services/request.interceptor.ts:16`

**How:**

1. Issue the session as a Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite cookie from the server.
2. Remove `localStorage` token handling and add CSRF protection and a strict CSP.

**Verification:** Confirm `document.cookie` and `localStorage` expose no session token after login.

**Reference:** [CWE-922: Insecure Storage of Sensitive Information](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html)

---

<a id="m-025"></a>
#### M-025 — Scope basket lookup to the caller

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-003](#f-003) — Missing object ownership checks on basket resources

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/basket.ts:19`

**How:**

1. Add the authenticated user's BasketId or UserId to the where clause of each basket lookup and update.
2. Compare the basket id to the authenticated user's basket id and return 403 for mismatches, including on BasketItems, checkout and coupon routes.

**Verification:** Using one user's token, request, check out, and apply a coupon to another user's basket id, and modify another user's basket item; confirm each returns 403.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

---

<a id="m-030"></a>
#### M-030 — Verify JWT signature before trusting identity

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — Chat identity trusted from unverified JWT decode

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:45`

**How:**

1. Replace `security.decode` with `security.verify` plus decode, or use the `isAuthorized` middleware on `/rest/chat`.
2. Reject requests whose token fails verification before any tool executes.

**Verification:** Send POST `/rest/chat` with a forged unsigned token for another user id and confirm 401 and no order data returned.

**Reference:** [CWE-347: Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html)

---

<a id="m-031"></a>
#### M-031 — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (F-014)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-014](#f-014) — JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/verify.ts:120`

**How:** Always pass `algorithms: ['RS256']` (or the project's chosen alg) as the third argument's options object to `jwt.verify`.

1. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHZ-005 scanner check; it reports no match at `routes/verify.ts:120`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-032"></a>
#### M-032 — Verify token signatures before trusting claims

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — OAuth implicit flow without state, nonce or audience

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**How:**

1. Switch to authorization code flow with PKCE and generate a per-request state and nonce stored in the session.
2. Exchange the code on the server and validate the ID token signature, iss, aud and nonce before creating a local session.

**Verification:** Replay a callback with a missing or mismatched state and a token issued for another client; both must be rejected.

---

<a id="m-033"></a>
#### M-033 — Stop deriving passwords from the email

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — Predictable password derived from OAuth email

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**How:**

1. Create OAuth accounts server-side from a validated provider identity, with a random credential that cannot be used for password login.
2. Authenticate OAuth users by server-side verification of the provider token instead of a derived password.
3. Reject password logins for accounts flagged as OAuth-only and remove client-side password derivation.

**Verification:** Attempt password login with base64 of the reversed email for an OAuth-created account and confirm it is rejected.

**Reference:** [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html)

---

<a id="m-034"></a>
#### M-034 — Use cryptographically secure random values

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-036](#w-036)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-330](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/330.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:53`

**How:** Use `crypto.randomBytes` / `crypto.randomUUID` / webcrypto `getRandomValues` for any security value.

1. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the CRYPTO-002 scanner check; it reports no match at `lib/insecurity.ts:53`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-035"></a>
#### M-035 — Add throttling and stronger reset verification

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-020](#f-020) — Password reset relies on security-question answer

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-013](#w-013)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) - Weak Password Recovery Mechanism

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Replace security-question-only recovery with a single-use, expiring token sent to the account email.
2. Apply per-account and per-IP rate limiting and lockout to the reset route.

**Verification:** Send 200 wrong answers for a known account and confirm requests are throttled (429) and that no answer alone resets the password.

**Reference:** [CWE-640: Weak Password Recovery Mechanism](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html)

---

<a id="m-037"></a>
#### M-037 — Commit lockfiles and install from them

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — Lockfiles disabled so every build resolves floating dependencies

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-037](#w-037)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `.npmrc:1`

**How:**

1. Remove lockfile suppression from the npmrc and commit `package-lock.json` for root and frontend.
2. Use npm ci `--ignore-scripts` in CI, release and `Dockerfile`, and pin global tools to exact versions.
3. Add a minimum release age policy for new package versions.

**Verification:** Run npm ci in a clean checkout and confirm CI fails when `package.json` and the lockfile diverge.

**Reference:** [CWE-829: Inclusion of Functionality from Untrusted Control Sphere](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

---

<a id="m-038"></a>
#### M-038 — Validate and bound wallet top-up amount

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-023](#f-023) — Wallet top-up accepts arbitrary client balance amount

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-20](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/wallet.ts:27`

**How:**

1. Validate balance as a positive number within a set maximum and reject others.
2. Credit the wallet only after a confirmed payment from the payment provider, not on the client-supplied amount.

**Verification:** Send PUT `/rest/wallet/balance` with balance -50 and 1e12 and confirm both are rejected with 400.

**Reference:** [CWE-20: Improper Input Validation](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html)

<a id="m-039"></a>
#### M-039 — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory (F-024)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-024](#f-024) — Path Traversal

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/dataErasure.ts:104`

**How:**

1. Render with an explicit object of known fields instead of spreading `req.body`.
2. Remove the layout option or map it to a fixed allowlist of templates.
3. Drop `req.body.layout` and the body spread from the render options.
4. If layouts are needed, map fixed names to view files.

**Verification:** POST `/dataerasure` with layout=`/etc/hostname` and confirm the response does not contain the file content.

---

<a id="m-040"></a>
#### M-040 — Confine zip entries to the target directory

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — Zip slip arbitrary file write in complaint upload

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:34`

**How:**

1. Resolve the entry path against the destination uploads/complaints directory and require it to start with that resolved directory plus a path separator.
2. Write to the validated absolute path instead of re-concatenating the raw entry name.
3. Reject the request when an entry fails the containment check.

**Verification:** Upload a zip containing an entry named ../..`/ftp/legal.md` and confirm the request is rejected, no file is written, and `ftp/legal.md` is unchanged.

---

<a id="m-041"></a>
#### M-041 — Remove dynamic evaluation of order data

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-026](#f-026) — B2B order payload evaluated by notevil sandbox

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Code Injection

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/b2bOrder.ts:23`

**How:**

1. Parse `orderLinesData` as JSON and validate it against a schema instead of evaluating it.
2. Remove the `notevil` dependency.
3. If evaluation is truly required, run it in an isolated process or worker without host access.

**Verification:** Post `orderLinesData` containing a function-constructor or function-call expression and confirm a 400 validation error, with valid JSON order lines still accepted.

**Reference:** [CWE-94: Improper Control of Generation of Code (Code Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

---

<a id="m-042"></a>
#### M-042 — Credit wallets only after a confirmed payment

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Wallet top-up amount is taken from the request body

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-472](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/472.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/wallet.ts:27`

**How:**

1. Validate balance as a positive bounded number and charge the card before incrementing.
2. Take the amount from a server-side payment result, not the body.

**Verification:** PUT with balance 1000000 without a payment and expect rejection; PUT with a negative value and expect 400.

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/xml.ts:35`

**How:**

1. Remove NOENT and DTDLOAD options and do not register filesystem input providers.
2. Reject documents containing DOCTYPE declarations.

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**How:**

1. Make the current password mandatory and reject the request when it is missing or does not match the stored hash.
2. Change the route to POST with credentials in the body instead of query parameters.

**Verification:** Call the endpoint without the current parameter and confirm a 401 response.

<a id="m-045"></a>
#### M-045 — Force UserId from the authenticated session

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:382`

**How:**

1. Add `security.appendUserId()` to these POST routes so UserId is server-set
2. Reject or strip client-supplied UserId and other privileged fields

**Verification:** POST with a foreign UserId and confirm the stored UserId is the caller's.

<a id="m-046"></a>
#### M-046 — Use typed query predicates instead of \$where

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-033](#f-033) — NoSQL JavaScript injection in product

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`

**How:**

1. Always coerce the id with `Number()` and reject NaN, removing the flag-dependent branch that skips numeric coercion.
2. Query with `reviewsCollection.find({ product: id })` instead of a \$where string.

**Verification:** Request `/rest/products/1`||true/reviews and with `id=1`; `sleep(3000)` and confirm a 400 response or an exact-match result, with no delay.

**Reference:** [CWE-943: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements in Data Query Logic (NoSQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html)

---

<a id="m-047"></a>
#### M-047 — Replace \$where string with a parameterised find

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — NoSQL JavaScript injection in order tracking \$where

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Drop the \$where string and query with `findOne({ orderId: String(id) })` using a string value.
2. Always apply the non-word character allow-list, independent of any challenge flag.

**Verification:** Request `/rest/track-order/`' || true || ' and confirm only the exact matching order or none is returned.

**Reference:** [CWE-943: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements in Data Query Logic (NoSQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html)

<a id="m-048"></a>
#### M-048 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (F-035)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-035](#f-035) — Pug template injection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-018](#w-018)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1336](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1336.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:87`

**How:**

1. Keep the template static and supply the username through the render locals so pug escapes it.
2. Remove string replacement of user-controlled values into the template source.
3. Compile the template once at startup, not per request.

**Verification:** Store usernames such as #{7*7} and a markup payload and confirm `/profile` shows them as inert text.

**Reference:** [CWE-1336: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements Used in a Template Engine (Server-Side Template Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1336.html)

- [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:206`

**How:**

1. Validate messages with a zod schema allowing only user role with string content and bounded length.
2. Keep conversation history server-side or reject non-user roles.

**Verification:** POST a messages array containing a system role and confirm a 400 response.

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`

2. Build the stars and caption in the template instead of by string concatenation.

<a id="m-052"></a>

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

2. Bind values with text interpolation, or let Angular sanitize [`innerHTML`] without bypass.

<a id="m-054"></a>

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

<a id="m-055"></a>
#### M-055 — Stop storing sensitive data in cleartext

**Addresses:**

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `models/card.ts:38`

**How:**

<a id="m-056"></a>
#### M-056 — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-043](#f-043) — Unsalted MD5 password hashing

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-036](#w-036)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `models/user.ts:76`

**How:**

1. Replace `security.hash` in the password setter with Argon2id or bcrypt using a per-password salt and a tuned cost.
2. Rehash existing `MD5` hashes at the next successful login.
3. Enforce a minimum length and strength policy on registration, change and reset.

**Verification:** Create a user and confirm the stored value is an Argon2id or bcrypt string that differs between identical passwords, and that login still succeeds.

<a id="m-059"></a>
#### M-059 — Enforce ownership on user lookup by id

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-046](#f-046) — Any authenticated user can read any user record by id

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:365`

**How:**

1. Add middleware that compares `req.params.id` with the authenticated user id and otherwise allows only the admin role.
2. Restrict the generated resource's returned attributes to non-sensitive fields, excluding `deluxeToken` and `lastLoginIp`.

**Verification:** Log in as user A, request GET `/api/Users/`&lt;user B id&gt;, and expect 403; confirm `deluxeToken` and `lastLoginIp` are absent from permitted responses.

<a id="m-060"></a>
#### M-060 — Validate and allowlist outbound request targets

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-047](#f-047) — Server-side request forgery in profile

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) - Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`

**How:**

1. Allow only http/https (https where possible) and resolve the host, rejecting loopback, link-local, private and metadata addresses.
2. Disable redirect following or re-validate each hop.
3. Validate the fetched content type and magic bytes as an image, reject svg, and cap size.
4. Do not store the raw URL on fetch failure.

**Verification:** Post `imageUrl`=`http://169.254.169.254/` and `imageUrl`=`http://127.0.0.1:3000/` to `/profile/image/url` with a session cookie and confirm both are rejected and nothing is written to uploads.

**Reference:** [CWE-918: Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html)

2. Set `maxOutputTokens`, cap message count and total input length, and add a per-user budget.

**How:**

2. Use finale `list.fetch.before` to scope results to the authenticated user

**Verification:** As a normal user, GET `/api/Complaints` and confirm only own records or 403.

<a id="m-070"></a>
#### M-070 — Guard product update route with role check

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-061](#f-061) — Unauthenticated product update

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing Authorization

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

1. Register `security.isAuthorized()` plus an admin role check on PUT `/api/Products/:id`, or `security.denyAll()` if updates are not needed.
2. Restrict updatable fields on the resource, for example price only for the admin role.

**Verification:** Send PUT `/api/Products/1` without a token and with a customer token; both must return 401 or 403.

---

<a id="m-071"></a>
#### M-071 — Enforce coupon limits in server code

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-062](#f-062) — Coupon tool discount policy enforced only in prompt

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**How:**

1. Add `z.number().min(1).max(10)` to the schema and re-check the bound inside `execute()`.
2. Verify a damaged order belonging to the authenticated caller server-side before issuing a coupon, or require human approval.

**Verification:** Invoke the tool with discount 50 and confirm it is rejected without producing a coupon.

**Reference:** [OWASP: Www Project Top 10 For Large Language Model Applications](https://owasp.org/www-project-top-10-for-large-language-model-applications/)

---

<a id="m-072"></a>
#### M-072 — Strip role from registration input

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-063](#f-063) — Registration accepts client-supplied role

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:420`

**How:**

1. Delete or reject role and other privileged fields in the registration middleware.
2. Assign the default customer role on the server.
3. Allowlist accepted registration fields.

**Verification:** POST `/api/Users` with role admin and confirm the created user has the customer role.

**Reference:** [CWE-915: Improperly Controlled Modification of Dynamically-Determined Object Attributes (Mass Assignment)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

<a id="m-073"></a>
#### M-073 — Restrict user listing to admin role

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-064](#f-064) — Missing role check on user list endpoint

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing Authorization

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/authenticatedUsers.ts:12`

**How:**

1. Add a server-side admin role check in front of the handler at route registration.
2. Return only the fields the privileged consumer needs rather than spreading `user.dataValues`.

**Verification:** Call the endpoint with a customer token and confirm HTTP 403.

<a id="m-018"></a>
#### M-018 — Sign and verify release artifacts

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-073](#f-073) — Missing Container Image Signing

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`

**How:** Add cosign signing step to release workflow; or use actions/attest-build-provenance - Unsigned container images cannot be verified for provenance - any registry intermediary could substitute them.

1. Add cosign signing step to release workflow; or use actions/attest-build-provenance
2. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the IAC-040 scanner check; it reports no match at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-019"></a>
#### M-019 — Disable untrusted package install scripts

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-075](#f-075) — Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-027](#w-027)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `Dockerfile:4`

**How:** Use `npm ci --ignore-scripts` and audit required postinstalls separately - Without `--ignore-scripts`, any compromised dependency publisher can execute postinstall code at build time.

1. Use `npm ci --ignore-scripts` and audit required postinstalls separately
2. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the IAC-005 scanner check; it reports no match at `Dockerfile:4`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-020"></a>
#### M-020 — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-077](#f-077) — Container base images referenced by tag instead of digest

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-026](#w-026)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) - Use of Unmaintained Third-Party Components

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `Dockerfile:1`

**How:** Pin base image to @sha256:`<digest>` - Tag-only base images (FROM node:24) can be silently substituted by a malicious publisher. Digest-pinning (@sha256:…) ensures the exact image bytes are used on every build.

1. Pin FROM lines in `Dockerfile` and `test/smoke/Dockerfile` to sha256 digests.
2. Use Renovate or Dependabot to refresh digests.

**Verification:** Run docker buildx imagetools inspect on each FROM and confirm the Dockerfiles carry @sha256 digests.

**Reference:** [CWE-829: Inclusion of Functionality from Untrusted Control Sphere](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

---

<a id="m-021"></a>
#### M-021 — Drop unnecessary privileges in build and runtime

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-104](#f-104) — Container Runs as Root

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1`

**How:** Add USER `<non-root-uid>` - Containers running as root expand container-escape blast radius.

1. Add USER `<non-root-uid>`
2. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the IAC-002 scanner check; it reports no match at `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-022"></a>
#### M-022 — Apply least-privilege permissions

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-105](#f-105) — Missing Workflow Permissions Block

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-023](#w-023)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:25`

**How:** Add `permissions: { contents: read }` at workflow root; elevate per-job only where needed - Without an explicit permissions block at workflow or job level, the job inherits the repository default (commonly write-all) for `GITHUB_TOKEN` - any compromised step can push code, create releases, or approve PRs.

1. Add `permissions: { contents: read }` at workflow root; elevate per-job only where needed
2. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the IAC-010 scanner check; it reports no match at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:25`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-076"></a>
#### M-076 — Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-067](#f-067) — OAuth implicit flow without state or nonce

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-024](#w-024)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) - Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**How:**

1. Switch to the authorization code flow with PKCE.
2. Generate and verify a random state and nonce per login attempt.

**Verification:** Inspect the authorization URL and confirm it uses `response_type=code` with state and `code_challenge`.

**Reference:** [datatracker.ietf.org: Rfc9700](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9700)

2. Add per-user failed-attempt lockout on the verify endpoint.

**Verification:** Submit repeated invalid codes for one user and confirm lockout after the configured limit.

<a id="m-078"></a>
#### M-078 — Validate exp, iss and aud in token verification

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:55`

**How:**

1. Use jsonwebtoken verify with algorithms `RS256`, issuer and audience in `verify()`.
2. Set issuer and audience when signing in `authorize()`.

<a id="m-079"></a>
#### M-079 — Pin RS256 when verifying tokens

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-070](#f-070) — JWT verification does not pin the signing algorithm

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:55`

**How:**

1. Call `jws.verify` with an explicit `RS256` algorithm and the public key.
2. Pass `algorithms: ['RS256']` to `expressJwt` and `jwt.verify`.

**Verification:** Submit tokens with alg none or `HS256` signed with the public key and confirm each is rejected.

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/order.ts:149`

**How:**

1. Use a conditional UPDATE that decrements only when balance >= `totalPrice` and check the affected row count.
2. Reject negative `totalPrice` before debiting.

**Verification:** Fire parallel checkouts against a wallet that can pay for one order and confirm only one succeeds.

<a id="m-085"></a>
#### M-085 — Validate type and size of fetched profile images

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-079](#f-079) — Fetched profile image stored without type

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) - Unrestricted Upload of File with Dangerous Type

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:30`

**How:**

1. Cap the downloaded bytes and verify the content with a `file-type` check as in the file upload route.
2. Drop svg from the allowed extensions or sanitize it.

**Verification:** Point `imageUrl` at a large non-image file and an SVG and confirm both are rejected.

<a id="m-086"></a>
#### M-086 — Validate profile image before use

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-080](#f-080) — CSP header built from user-controlled profile image

- [CWE-644](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/644.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:88`

**How:**

1. Use a fixed CSP and do not interpolate user data into it.
2. If an image source is needed, validate it as a strict URL with an allowlisted scheme and host and reject semicolons and whitespace.

**Verification:** Store a `profileImage` containing ; script-src 'unsafe-inline' and confirm the response CSP header is unchanged.

**Reference:** [CWE-644](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/644.html)

<a id="m-087"></a>

1. Overwrite `req.body.UserId` from the verified session, or remove it for anonymous posts.
2. Reject requests whose body UserId differs from the caller.

<a id="m-088"></a>
#### M-088 — Bind security answer owner to caller

**Addresses:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

1. Register `security.isAuthorized()` and `security.appendUserId()` on POST `/api/SecurityAnswers` and overwrite `body.UserId`.
2. Restrict creatable fields to answer and SecurityQuestionId.

**Verification:** POST an answer with a different UserId in the body; the stored UserId must be the caller's.

<a id="m-089"></a>
#### M-089 — Validate review id as a string before querying

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-083](#f-083) — NoSQL operator injection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/likeProductReviews.ts:25`

**How:**

1. Require typeof id === 'string' and reject other types with a 400.
2. Validate the id format before querying.
3. Pass only the validated scalar into the `findOne` and update predicates.

**Verification:** POST id as `{"$ne":-1}` and confirm a 400 response and unchanged review data.

<a id="m-090"></a>
#### M-090 — Scope review update to the owning author

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-084](#f-084) — Review update lacks ownership check

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Require `req.body.id` to be a string and match { _id: id, author: `user.data.email` }.
2. Remove `multi: true`.

**Verification:** As user A, PATCH another user's review id and with `{"$ne":-1}`; confirm zero documents are modified.

**Reference:** [CWE-943: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements in Data Query Logic (NoSQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html)

<a id="m-091"></a>
#### M-091 — Isolate stored content from model instructions

**Prevents CWEs:**

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:149`

**How:**

1. Delimit review text as untrusted data and strip instruction-like content.
2. Require server-side authorization for consequential tools regardless of model output.

<a id="m-092"></a>
#### M-092 — Stop trusting API product description HTML

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-086](#f-086) — Stored XSS

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`

**How:**

1. Remove the trust-HTML loop so Angular sanitizes descriptions on render.
2. Sanitize product descriptions on the server at write time, using an allow-list if limited markup is needed.
3. Restrict who can edit product data on the server.

**Verification:** Store a description containing a script payload and confirm it is stripped or not executed on the product listing.

**Reference:** [CWE-79: Improper Neutralization of Input During Web Page Generation (XSS)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `models/securityAnswer.ts:45`

**How:**

1. Load the HMAC key from environment or a secret manager rather than source.
2. Use a per-record salt with a slow KDF for answers.

<a id="m-095"></a>
#### M-095 — Validate file name after normalization in FTP server

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-089](#f-089) — FTP file allow-list bypass

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/fileServer.ts:33`

**How:**

1. Reject any file name containing a null byte or encoded null before the allow-list check.
2. Perform the extension check on the final name passed to `sendFile` and remove `cutOffPoisonNullByte`.

**Verification:** Request `/ftp/package.json.bak%2500.md` and confirm a 403 response.

**How:**

1. Reject any file name containing a null byte or percent-encoded null byte.
2. Apply the extension allowlist to the final name that is passed to `sendFile`.

**Verification:** Request `/ftp/package.json.bak%2500.md` and confirm a 403 response.

**How:**

1. Add `z.number().max(10)` and reject values above the cap in execute.
2. Verify order ownership, damage status and rejected return server-side before issuing a coupon, and require approval.

**Verification:** Invoke `generateCoupon` with discount 50 through `/rest/chat` and confirm the tool rejects it.

**Reference:** [OWASP GenAI: LLM062025 Excessive Agency](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm062025-excessive-agency/)

---

<a id="m-119"></a>
#### M-119 — Enforce coupon policy in server-side code

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-115](#f-115) — Chatbot coupon limits enforced only by prompt text

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**How:**

1. Add `z.number().max(10).min(1)` to the discount schema and re-check the bound in `execute()`.
2. Verify order ownership, damage state and rejected-return status in code before issuing a coupon, and require authentication on `/rest/chat`.

**Verification:** Send a chat request asking for a 50% coupon and confirm the tool call is rejected server-side.

---

<a id="m-120"></a>
#### M-120 — Bind address updates to the authenticated owner

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-116](#f-116) — Address update lacks ownership check

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:450`

**How:**

1. Before the finale update, load the Address by :id and compare its UserId to the token's user id; reject with 403 otherwise.
2. Alternatively add a finale `update.fetch.before` hook restricting the where clause to the caller's UserId.

**Verification:** As user A, PUT `/api/Addresss/`&lt;id of user B's address>; expect 403/404 and unchanged data.

_No P4 mitigations._

## 11. Out of Scope

### Not Covered by This Method

An automated, AI-assisted threat model built from the repository's source code and configuration - static analysis only, no dynamic or penetration testing. It models the system as built rather than as designed. It does not replace a threat-modeling session with the team: business context, design intent, runtime behaviour and production configuration are not covered, and each finding still needs confirmation in the deployed system.

- Design intent and the reasoning behind it - no design documents, ADRs or workshop context are read.
- Business processes and user journeys that leave the code.
- Runtime behaviour, deployment topology and production-only configuration.
- External and organizational controls.

Treat this report as review input, not sign-off.

### Excluded from This Assessment

The following items are **explicitly excluded** from this threat model. Findings against these areas should be tracked separately.

- Third-party hosted dependencies and SaaS endpoints
- Browser runtime vulnerabilities and end-user device security
- Operating system kernel and container runtime
- Underlying network infrastructure (DNS, BGP, ISP)
- Physical security of hosting facilities
- Opt-in threat actors, not assessed: malicious insiders with repository or pipeline access; malicious insiders with production access; attackers holding a user's device. Enable them with `enable:` in `.appsec/actors.yaml`.

<a id="appendix-verification-items"></a>
## Appendix: Verification Items

Verification checks fixed security properties item by item (catalog version 6). Results per item and component in this run: 32 findings · 1 controls · 18 no finding · 14 undecidable · 178 not applicable. A control means that the examined code enforces the property at every place it applies; a partial, undecidable or no-finding result is never a control, and neither is an item this run did not examine.

### Contribution of analysis steps

Source anchors count locations, not independent weaknesses. Different anchors can describe the same problem. Semantic novelty and missing measurements are shown as unknown. Review confirmations do not establish an executed attack.

| Step | Additional locations | Repeated locations | New distinct problems | Stronger evidence | Contradictions | Recorded cost (USD) |
|---------------|--------------------:|------------------:|---------------------:|-----------------:|--------------:|-------------------:|
| Survey | 95 | unknown | unknown | unknown | unknown | 0.87 |
| Verification | 28 | 35 | unknown | unknown | unknown | 3.88 |
| Evidence review | unknown | unknown | 0 | 149 | 37 | 2.14 |
| Abuse cases | 1 | unknown | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.94 |

Verification is compared with the original survey, including locations preserved inside consolidated findings. Abuse cases are compared with the findings immediately before their checks. Evidence review counts its recorded decisions. Costs include charged failed attempts when the run can attribute them; subscription use or unavailable accounting is unknown.

<a id="appendix-a-vektor-taxonomy"></a>
## Appendix A — Attack Vector Taxonomy

This appendix defines the attacker-starting-position labels used in the Top Threats table and throughout [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register). Each label answers the question *what does the attacker need before the exploit begins?*

<a id="vektor-internet-anon"></a>
### Internet Anon

**Attacker position:** Unauthenticated attacker from the public internet · **Breach distance:** 1

**Preconditions:**

- Endpoint is reachable from the internet (no IP allowlist, no VPN)
- No authentication middleware blocks the request

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-user"></a>
### Internet User

**Attacker position:** Any authenticated low-privilege user (valid JWT / session) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker has signed up or otherwise obtained a valid user session
- Endpoint is behind auth but not behind role/admin checks

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A06:2025, A02:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-priv-user"></a>
### Internet Priv User

**Attacker position:** Authenticated admin-level user (JWT with admin role or equivalent) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker holds admin credentials or has elevated privileges
- Endpoint gated on admin role but still exploitable once reached

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025

<a id="vektor-victim-required"></a>
### Victim-Required

**Attacker position:** Attacker needs victim interaction - social engineering, crafted link, or live session · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Victim must click a link, load a page, or have an active session
- Applies to XSS, CSRF, click-jacking, open redirect

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [CWE-1021](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1021.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025

<a id="vektor-build-time"></a>
### Build-Time

**Attacker position:** Attacker controls a build input - CI runner, dependency, base image, or external data fetched during build · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Compromise of a dependency, registry, or base image
- OR compromise of a CI runner with write access to artifacts

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [CWE-1039](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1039.html) · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A08:2025

<a id="vektor-repo-read"></a>
### Repo-Read

**Attacker position:** Attacker gains read access to source repository (leaked clone, forked fork, insider, compromised developer workstation) · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Read access to the source tree at or after commit time
- No runtime exploit needed - the vulnerability is the content of the repo

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [CWE-540](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/540.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A04:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-n-a"></a>
### Architectural (no runtime entry point)

**Attacker position:** Architectural / meta-finding - no runtime entry point, the finding describes a design defect aggregating multiple code-level findings

**Preconditions:**

- Finding is AF-NNN (architectural) rather than F-NNN (code-level)
